{"qimg":{"src":"2019-P2-Q13","w":1106,"h":1331},"n":"13","paperNum":"2","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2019","strand":"Properties of shapes","subtopic":"Angles","marks":2,"intro":"Here is a sketch of a triangle. It is not drawn to scale.\nOne angle is 35°, and the side opposite is 8cm.","ref":"6G3a","text":"Draw the full-size triangle accurately below.\nUse an angle measurer (protractor) and a ruler.\nOne line has been drawn for you.","answer":"Award TWO marks for a completed triangle that has all of the following three points:\n• an angle in the range 33° to 37° inclusive for the angle marked 35°\n• an angle in the range 88° to 92° inclusive for the right angle\n• the triangle has been drawn on an 8cm line (either on the given line or a line drawn), provided they have constructed both angles within the tolerance of the line 7.9cm to 8.1cm.","markscheme":"Accept drawings where any side has been extended past a vertex.\nWhen considering whether the triangle is completed, do not accept a quadrilateral or another shape drawn, OR a curved line used to complete the shape, OR sides not meeting to form a vertex.\nIf the answer is incorrect, award ONE mark for a completed triangle and two of the three points correct."},
{"qimg":{"src":"2019-P2-Q14","w":1106,"h":408},"n":"14","paperNum":"2","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2019","strand":"Number and place value","subtopic":"Rounding","marks":2,"ref":"5N4","text":"Complete the table.\nRound 39,476\nto the nearest 10,000\nto the nearest 1,000\nto the nearest 100","answer":"Award TWO marks for the correct completion of the three numbers in the table, as shown:","markscheme":"Do not accept 9,000 or 500 for the second and third entries.\nIf the answer is incorrect, award ONE mark for any two of the numbers rounded correctly.","ansImg":{"src":"2019-P2-Q14-table","w":356,"h":224}},
{"qimg":{"src":"2019-P2-Q15","w":1106,"h":813},"n":"15","paperNum":"2","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2019","strand":"Ratio and proportion","subtopic":"Percentages of amounts","marks":1,"intro":"Amina asked 60 children to choose their favourite flavour of jelly.\nRaspberry: 12, Lemon: 8, Orange: 15, Blackcurrant: 25, Total: 60","ref":"5F12/5S1","text":"What percentage of the 60 children chose orange?","answer":"25","markscheme":""},
{"qimg":{"src":"2019-P2-Q16","w":1106,"h":250},"n":"16","paperNum":"2","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2019","strand":"Calculation","subtopic":"Order of operations","marks":1,"ref":"6C9","text":"Write the missing number.\n6 + 2 × 2 − ☐ = 6","answer":"4","markscheme":""},
{"qimg":{"src":"2019-P2-Q17","w":1106,"h":943},"n":"17","paperNum":"2","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2019","strand":"Measurement","subtopic":"Area","marks":2,"intro":"These two shapes have the same perimeter.\nregular hexagon (not actual size), square\nThe length of each side of the hexagon is 8 centimetres.","ref":"6M7a/5M7b","text":"Calculate the area of the square.","answer":"Award TWO marks for the correct answer of 144","markscheme":"If the answer is incorrect, award ONE mark for evidence of an appropriate method, e.g.\n• 8 × 6 = 48\n48 ÷ 4 = 13 (error)\n13 × 13 = 169\nOR\nAward ONE mark for:\n• evidence for the side length of the square calculated correctly, i.e. 12\nAdditional guidance:\nAnswer need not be obtained for the award of ONE mark."},
{"qimg":{"src":"2019-P2-Q18","w":1106,"h":661},"n":"18","paperNum":"2","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2019","strand":"Calculation","subtopic":"Prime numbers","marks":1,"ref":"5C5c","text":"Circle the prime number.\n95   89   87\nExplain how you know the other numbers are not prime.","answer":"Award ONE mark for a correct explanation of why the 95 AND 87 are NOT prime, e.g.\n• 87 is divisible by 3 and/or 29 AND 95 is divisible by 5 and/or 19\n• 87 is in the 3 times table AND 95 is in the 5 times table","markscheme":"No mark is awarded for circling '89' alone.\nBoth non-primes must be explained correctly for the award of the mark.\nDo not accept vague or incomplete explanations, e.g. '87 is divisible by 3' (incomplete).\nDo not accept explanations which include incorrect mathematics or incorrect information relevant to the explanation."},
{"qimg":{"src":"2019-P2-Q19","w":1106,"h":526},"n":"19","paperNum":"2","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2019","strand":"Measurement","subtopic":"Converting units","marks":2,"intro":"A machine pours 250 millilitres of juice every 4 seconds.","ref":"6R1/6M5","text":"How many litres of juice does the machine pour every minute?","answer":"Award TWO marks for the correct answer of 3.75","markscheme":"Accept for TWO marks, 3,750ml for final answer in working and the answer box blank OR 3,750 in the answer box where the litres has been replaced with millilitres.\nIf the answer is incorrect, award ONE mark for evidence of an appropriate method, e.g.\n• 60 ÷ 4 = 15\n250 × 15 = 3750\n3750 ml ÷ 1000 =\nAdditional guidance:\nAccept for ONE mark 3,750 litres (l) in the answer box OR the final answer in working and answer box blank.\nAnswer need not be obtained for the award of ONE mark."},
{"qimg":{"src":"2019-P2-Q20","w":1106,"h":689},"n":"20","paperNum":"2","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2019","strand":"Ratio and proportion","subtopic":"Percentages of amounts","marks":2,"ref":"6F11","text":"Tick the fractions that are equal to 20%.\n1/20   20/40   1/5   3/15   2/100","answer":"Award TWO marks for two boxes ticked correctly, as shown:","markscheme":"Accept alternative unambiguous positive indication of the correct answer, e.g. Y.\nIf the answer is incorrect, award ONE mark for:\n• only one box ticked correctly and no incorrect boxes ticked\n• two boxes ticked correctly and one incorrect box ticked.","ansImg":{"src":"2019-P2-Q20-ticks","w":210,"h":558}},
{"qimg":{"src":"2019-P2-Q21","w":1106,"h":923},"n":"21","paperNum":"2","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2019","strand":"Properties of shapes","subtopic":"Quadrilaterals","marks":1,"intro":"Adam has this rectangular piece of card. It is marked with grid lines.\nAdam makes two straight cuts along the grid lines.\nThe two cuts divide the rectangle into 3 shapes:\n• 2 squares of different size, and\n• 1 rectangle.","ref":"6G3a/5C5d","text":"Using the grid lines, draw two lines that show where Adam could have made his cuts.\nUse a ruler.","answer":"Rectangle divided, as shown (any of four equivalent orientations accepted):","markscheme":"Accept slight inaccuracies in drawing provided the intention is clear.","ansImg":{"src":"2019-P2-Q21-answer","w":465,"h":339}},
{"qimg":{"src":"2019-P2-Q22","w":1106,"h":1404},"n":"22","paperNum":"2","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2019","strand":"Statistics","subtopic":["Line graphs","Mean"],"marks":3,"intro":"This graph shows the maximum temperature for five days.\nMon: 8.1°C, Tue: 9.3°C, Wed: 11.9°C, Thu: 11.8°C, Fri: 12.4°C","ref":"5S2/3F1b","text":"(a) For what fraction of the five days was the maximum temperature below 10°C?\n(b) What was the mean maximum temperature, to one decimal place?","answer":"(a) 2/5&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;(b) Award TWO marks for the correct answer of 10.7","markscheme":"22a: Accept equivalent fractions and decimals e.g. 4/10 and 0.4\n22b: If the answer is incorrect, award ONE mark for evidence of an appropriate method, e.g.\n• 8.1 + 9.3 + 11.9 + 11.8 + 12.4 = 53.5\n53.5 ÷ 5\nAdditional guidance:\nAnswer need not be obtained for the award of ONE mark.\nAny correct rounding or truncating does not negate an appropriate method.\nAny value which does not result from correct rounding or truncating implies an additional step not shown."},
{"qimg":{"src":"2019-P2-Q23","w":1106,"h":1053},"n":"23","paperNum":"2","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2019","strand":"Measurement","subtopic":"Volume","marks":2,"intro":"Amina made this cuboid using centimetre cubes: 6cm long, 4cm tall, 3cm wide.\nStefan makes a cuboid that is 5cm longer, 5cm taller and 5cm wider than Amina's cuboid.","ref":"6M8a/6C8","text":"What is the difference between the number of cubes in Amina's and Stefan's cuboids?","answer":"Award TWO marks for the correct answer of 720","markscheme":"If the answer is incorrect, award ONE mark for evidence of an appropriate method, e.g.\n• 3 × 4 × 6 = 72\n8 × 9 × 11 = 792\n792 – 72 =\nAward ONE mark for sight of 792\nAdditional guidance:\nAnswer need not be obtained for the award of ONE mark."},
{"qimg":{"src":"2019-P3-Q1","w":1106,"h":623},"n":"1","paperNum":"3","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2019","strand":"Measurement","subtopic":"Money problems","marks":1,"intro":"The original price of this car is £8,999\nSale: £1,100 off","ref":"4N2b/3N2b","text":"What is the sale price of the car?","answer":"£7,899","markscheme":""},
{"qimg":{"src":"2019-P3-Q2","w":1106,"h":636},"n":"2","paperNum":"3","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2019","strand":"Number and place value","subtopic":["Place value","Rounding"],"marks":2,"intro":"3,576,219","ref":"6N2/6N4","text":"(a) Which digit is in the ten thousands place?\n(b) Round 3,576,219 to the nearest million.","answer":"(a) 7&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;(b) 4,000,000","markscheme":"2a: Do not accept 70,000 or 70 thousands.\n2b: Accept 4 million or four million.\nDo not accept the answer 4"},
{"qimg":{"src":"2019-P3-Q3","w":1106,"h":894},"n":"3","paperNum":"3","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2019","strand":"Algebra","subtopic":"Finding unknown values","marks":1,"intro":"Dev says, 'I had £10. I gave some money away.'\na is the amount of money, in pounds, that Dev gave away.","ref":"6A1","text":"Which expression shows how much money Dev has left?\nTick one.\n10 + a\n10 ÷ a\na − 10\n10 − a\na × 10","answer":"Award ONE mark for the correct box ticked, as shown:","markscheme":"Accept alternative unambiguous positive indication of the correct answer, e.g. Y.","ansImg":{"src":"2019-P3-Q3-ticks","w":300,"h":546}},

{"qimg":{"src":"2019-P3-Q5","w":1106,"h":261},"n":"5","paperNum":"3","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2019","strand":"Number and place value","subtopic":"Place value","marks":1,"ref":"3C4/3N3","text":"Write the missing digits to make this addition correct.\n☐2 + 7☐ = 200","answer":"Addition completed, as shown:","markscheme":"All numbers must be correct for the award of the mark.","ansImg":{"src":"2019-P3-Q5-answer","w":444,"h":78}},
{"qimg":{"src":"2019-P3-Q6","w":1106,"h":887},"n":"6","paperNum":"3","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2019","strand":"Measurement","subtopic":"Money problems","marks":2,"intro":"John buys one toy car (£1.49) and one pack of stickers (£1.64).\nHe pays with a £10 note.","ref":"4F10b/4M9","text":"How much change does John get?","answer":"Award TWO marks for the correct answer of £6.87","markscheme":"If the answer is incorrect, award ONE mark for evidence of an appropriate method, e.g.\n• £1.49 + £1.64 = £3.13\n£10 − £3.13 =\nOR\n• £10 − £1.49 = £8.51\n£8.51 − £1.64 =\nAdditional guidance:\nAnswer need not be obtained for the award of ONE mark.\nAccept for ONE mark an answer of £687 OR £687p as evidence of an appropriate method."},
{"qimg":{"src":"2019-P3-Q7","w":1106,"h":1351},"n":"7","paperNum":"3","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2019","strand":"Calculation","subtopic":["Missing number problems","Tables"],"marks":2,"intro":"This picture shows the masses of eight kittens: 305g, 375g, 310g, 255g, 275g, 410g, 360g, 345g.","ref":"3M1b/4S2","text":"(a) What is the difference in mass between the heaviest kitten and the lightest kitten?\nThe masses of the kittens are to be put in four groups.\n(b) Write the missing numbers in the table. One has been done for you.","answer":"(a) 155&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;(b) Table completed with three correct numbers, as shown:","markscheme":"7b: All three numbers must be correct for the award of the mark.\nDo not accept tally marks on their own.","ansImg":{"src":"2019-P3-Q7b-table","w":464,"h":362}},
{"qimg":{"src":"2019-P3-Q8","w":1106,"h":639},"n":"8","paperNum":"3","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2019","strand":"Calculation","subtopic":"Written addition and subtraction","marks":2,"intro":"Ken is playing a game. He has 4,289 points.\nThen he scores another 355 points.\nKen's target is 6,000 points.","ref":"4C4/4C2","text":"How many more points does Ken need to reach his target?","answer":"Award TWO marks for the correct answer of 1,356","markscheme":"If the answer is incorrect, award ONE mark for evidence of an appropriate method, e.g.\n• 4289 + 355 = 4644\n6000 – 4644 =\nOR\n• 6000 – 4289 – 355 =\nOR\n• 6000 − 4289 = 1711\n1711 − 355 =\nAdditional guidance:\nAnswer need not be obtained for the award of ONE mark."},
{"qimg":{"src":"2019-P3-Q9","w":1106,"h":557},"n":"9","paperNum":"3","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2019","strand":"Statistics","subtopic":"Pictograms","marks":1,"intro":"This pictogram shows the number of satellites above the Earth in 2016.\nEach symbol represents 1,000 satellites.","ref":"4S2/4N4a","text":"How many satellites were above the Earth in 2016?","answer":"2,250","markscheme":"Do not accept 2000 1/4 OR 2 1/4 OR 2.25"},
{"qimg":{"src":"2019-P3-Q10","w":1106,"h":1334},"n":"10","paperNum":"3","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2019","strand":"Position and direction","subtopic":"Coordinates","marks":2,"intro":"On the grid there are three points joined by two lines.\nLara plots another point on the grid at (−1, 2). She joins the points to make a quadrilateral.","ref":"6P3/4P3b","text":"(a) Complete Lara's quadrilateral on the grid. Use a ruler.\nThen Lara translates the quadrilateral 4 squares to the right.\n(b) Draw the quadrilateral in its new position on the grid.","answer":"(a) Quadrilateral completed, as shown:\n[[img:2019-P3-Q10a-answer:612:618]]\n(b) Quadrilateral translated correctly, as shown:\n[[img:2019-P3-Q10b-answer:612:615]]","markscheme":"Accept slight inaccuracies in drawing provided the intention is clear.\n10b: Award ONE mark if the answer to (b) is a quadrilateral with sides drawn and is a correct translation of their answer to (a)."},
{"qimg":{"src":"2019-P3-Q11","w":1106,"h":736},"n":"11","paperNum":"3","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2019","strand":"Calculation","subtopic":["Prime numbers","Multiples"],"marks":2,"intro":"Here are five numbers.\n2   3   4   5   6\nThe number 2 has been written on the correct cards for you.","ref":"6C5","text":"Write each number on the correct cards.\nPrime numbers | Factors of 12 | Factors of 15","answer":"Award TWO marks for all four given numbers placed completely correctly 7 times, as shown:","markscheme":"Accept the numbers in any order.\nIgnore any additional numbers not given in the question.\nIf the answer is incorrect, award ONE mark for three of the given numbers all placed completely correctly.","ansImg":{"src":"2019-P3-Q11-answer","w":693,"h":282}},
{"qimg":{"src":"2019-P3-Q12","w":1106,"h":451},"n":"12","paperNum":"3","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2019","strand":"Ratio and proportion","subtopic":"Proportion problems","marks":1,"intro":"Amina's bed is 190cm in length and 91cm in width.\nShe is making a one-tenth scale model of the bed.","ref":"6R3","text":"What are the length and width of Amina's model?","answer":"length = 19 cm; width = 9.1 cm","markscheme":"Refer to the guidance on marking answers involving measures."},
{"qimg":{"src":"2019-P3-Q13","w":1106,"h":975},"n":"13","paperNum":"3","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2019","strand":"Properties of shapes","subtopic":"Angles","marks":1,"intro":"Kirsty says, 'When you double the size of an acute angle, you always get an obtuse angle.'","ref":"4G4","text":"Explain why Kirsty is not correct.","answer":"An explanation that includes a correct counter example, e.g.\n• When you double 10° it is not obtuse\n• 2 × 27° = 54°\n• Double 45° is a right angle not obtuse\nOR\nAn explanation that demonstrates where the statement in the question is not correct, e.g.\n• If the acute angle is less than 45° then doubling it will be less than 90°, so it won't be obtuse (more than 90°).","markscheme":"Do not accept vague or incomplete explanations, e.g. 'Sometimes it will be acute' or 'Some acute angles are half an obtuse angle, but not all'.\nDo not accept explanations which include incorrect mathematics or incorrect information relevant to the explanation."},
{"qimg":{"src":"2019-P3-Q14","w":1106,"h":233},"n":"14","paperNum":"3","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2019","strand":"Measurement","subtopic":"Converting units","marks":1,"ref":"3M4e","text":"How many days are there in September, October and November altogether?","answer":"91","markscheme":""},
{"qimg":{"src":"2019-P3-Q15","w":1106,"h":759},"n":"15","paperNum":"3","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2019","strand":"Measurement","subtopic":"Converting units","marks":1,"intro":"The International Space Station orbits the Earth at a height of 250 miles.\nUse 8 kilometres equals 5 miles.","ref":"6M6/6R1","text":"What is the height of the International Space Station in kilometres?","answer":"400","markscheme":""},
{"qimg":{"src":"2019-P3-Q16","w":1106,"h":805},"n":"16","paperNum":"3","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2019","strand":"Measurement","subtopic":"Money problems","marks":2,"intro":"potatoes £1.50 per kg, carrots £1.80 per kg.\nJack buys 1 1/2 kg of potatoes and 1/2 kg of carrots.","ref":"5M9c/5M9a","text":"How much change does he get from £5?","answer":"Award TWO marks for the correct answer of £1.85","markscheme":"If the answer is incorrect, award ONE mark for evidence of an appropriate method, e.g.\n• 1 1/2 × £1.50 = £2.25\n1/2 of £1.80 = 70p (error)\n£2.25 + 70p = £2.95\n£5 − £2.95 =\nOR\n• sight of £3.15 OR 315p as evidence of evaluating the correct cost of the potatoes and carrots.\nAdditional guidance:\nAnswer need not be obtained for the award of ONE mark.\nAccept for ONE mark an answer of £185 or £185p as evidence of an appropriate method."},
{"qimg":{"src":"2019-P3-Q17","w":1106,"h":496},"n":"17","paperNum":"3","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2019","strand":"Algebra","subtopic":"Finding unknown values","marks":1,"intro":"x + 2y = 20\nx and y are whole numbers less than 10","ref":"6A4","text":"What could x and y be?","answer":"Award ONE mark for any pair of whole numbers less than 10 that satisfy the equation, i.e.\nx = 8 AND y = 6\nOR x = 6 AND y = 7\nOR x = 4 AND y = 8\nOR x = 2 AND y = 9","markscheme":""},
{"qimg":{"src":"2019-P3-Q18","w":1106,"h":619},"n":"18","paperNum":"3","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2019","strand":"Fractions, decimals and percentages","subtopic":"Ordering fractions","marks":2,"ref":"5F3","text":"Tick the fractions less than 5/8\n1/2   2/8   3/4   7/16   24/32","answer":"Award TWO marks for three boxes ticked correctly, as shown:","markscheme":"Accept alternative unambiguous positive indication of the correct answer, e.g. Y.\nAward ONE mark for:\n• only two boxes ticked correctly and no incorrect boxes ticked\nOR\n• three boxes ticked correctly and one incorrect box ticked.","ansImg":{"src":"2019-P3-Q18-ticks","w":195,"h":561}},
{"qimg":{"src":"2019-P3-Q19","w":1106,"h":1047},"n":"19","paperNum":"3","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2019","strand":"Calculation","subtopic":"Missing number problems","marks":3,"intro":"Layla makes jewellery to sell at a school fair.\nEach bracelet has 53 beads. She makes 68 bracelets.\nEach necklace has 105 beads. She makes 34 necklaces.","ref":"6C8","text":"How many beads does Layla use altogether?","answer":"Award THREE marks for the correct answer of 7,174","markscheme":"If the answer is incorrect, award TWO marks for evidence of an appropriate complete method which contains no more than one arithmetic error, e.g.\n53 × 68 = 3504 (error)\n105 × 34 = 3570\n3,504 + 3,570 = 7,074\nAward ONE mark for evidence of an appropriate method with more than one arithmetic error.\nOR sight of 3,604 as evidence of long multiplication step (68 × 53) completed correctly.\nOR sight of 3,570 as evidence of long multiplication step (105 × 34) completed correctly.\nAdditional guidance:\nAnswer need not be obtained for the award of ONE mark.\nA misread of a number may affect the award of marks. No marks are awarded if there is more than one misread or if the mathematics is simplified."},
{"qimg":{"src":"2019-P3-Q20","w":1106,"h":1193},"n":"20","paperNum":"3","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2019","strand":"Calculation","subtopic":"Long division","marks":2,"intro":"Adam is making booklets.\nEach booklet must have 34 sheets of paper.\nHe has 2 packets of paper. There are 500 sheets of paper in each packet.","ref":"6C7b","text":"How many complete booklets can Adam make from 2 packets of paper?","answer":"Award TWO marks for the correct answer of 29","markscheme":"If the answer is incorrect, award ONE mark for evidence of an appropriate method, e.g.\n• 2 × 500 = 1,000\n1,000 ÷ 34 =\nOR\n• 2 × 500 ÷ 34 =\nAdditional guidance:\nAnswer need not be obtained for the award of ONE mark.\nAnswer does not need to have been rounded or rounded correctly for the award of ONE mark.\nDo not accept a trial and improvement method."},
{"qimg":{"src":"2019-P3-Q21","w":1106,"h":1064},"n":"21","paperNum":"3","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2019","strand":"Position and direction","subtopic":"Coordinates","marks":2,"intro":"ABDE is a rectangle on coordinate axes. The sides of the rectangle are parallel to the axes.\nA is (25, 30). Point C, the centre of the rectangle, is (40, 22).","ref":"5G2a/4P3a","text":"What are the coordinates of B and D?","answer":"(a) B is (55, 30)&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;(b) D is (55, 14)","markscheme":"If B and D are incorrect, ONE mark may be given for the correct y coordinate for both B and D and the same x coordinate (incorrect) for both points, i.e. D is (same x as B, 14)"},
{"qimg":{"src":"2019-P3-Q22","w":1106,"h":741},"n":"22","paperNum":"3","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2019","strand":"Properties of shapes","subtopic":"Quadrilaterals","marks":1,"intro":"Six identical right-angled triangles are arranged to make a rectangle.\nOne short side is 7cm.","ref":"6G2a/5G2a","text":"Calculate the length of the rectangle.","answer":"10.5 (cm)","markscheme":"Accept 10 1/2"},
{"qimg":{"src":"2019-P3-Q23","w":1106,"h":1146},"n":"23","paperNum":"3","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2019","strand":"Ratio and proportion","subtopic":"Proportion problems","marks":1,"intro":"The distance from point P to point R is 800 metres.\nThe distance from point P to point Q is 4 times the distance from point Q to point R.\nOlivia says, 'It is 600 metres from point P to point Q.'","ref":"6R1","text":"Explain why Olivia is not correct.","answer":"An explanation that gives the correct values for PQ and/or QR, e.g.\n• PQ = 640m\n• QR is 160, 160 times 4 is not 600m\nOR\nAn explanation recognising PR is 800m and must be 5 times QR, e.g.\n• the total distance is 800m. Divide by 5 to give 160 for distance between Q and R, so P and Q is 4 × 160 = 640m (not 600m)\nOR\nAn explanation that PQ is not 600m, e.g.\n• if it was 600m then the shorter distance would be 200m if added to make 800m, 600m is 3 times 200, not 4 times","markscheme":"Do not accept vague, incomplete or incorrect explanations, e.g. 'Olivia is not correct because you can't divide 600 by 4 like you can for 800'.\nDo not accept explanations which include incorrect mathematics or incorrect information relevant to the explanation."},

{"qimg":{"src":"2022-P2-Q18","w":1088,"h":912},"n":"18","paperNum":"2","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2022","strand":"Calculation","subtopic":"Written addition and subtraction","marks":2,"ref":"5C4","intro":"This sign shows the number of empty spaces on each level of a car park at 10am.\nEmpty Spaces\nLevel 2: 511\nLevel 1: 268\nIn this car park, each level has 800 spaces.","text":"What is the total number of cars parked in the car park at 10am?","answer":"Award TWO marks for the correct answer of 821","markscheme":"If the answer is incorrect, award ONE mark for evidence of an appropriate method, e.g.\n• 800 × 2 = 1600\n511 + 268 = 779\n1600 − 779\nOR\n• 800 − 511 = 289\n800 − 268 = 542 (error)\n542 + 289\nOR\n• 800 − 511 − 268 = 23 (error)\n800 + 23\nAdditional guidance:\nAnswer need not be obtained for the award of ONE mark."},

{"qimg":{"src":"2022-P2-Q20","w":1088,"h":948},"n":"20","paperNum":"2","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2022","strand":"Calculation","subtopic":"Long multiplication","marks":2,"ref":"5C7a/6C8","intro":"There are 432 places at a dance school.\nThere are two age groups.\nThis table shows the number of classes and the number of pupils in each class for each age group at the moment.\nAge 7–12: 15 classes, 16 pupils each\nAge 13–18: 10 classes, 18 pupils each","text":"How many more pupils can join the dance school?","answer":"Award TWO marks for the correct answer of 12","markscheme":"If the answer is incorrect, award ONE mark for evidence of an appropriate complete method with no more than one arithmetic error, e.g.\n• 16 × 15 = 210 (error)\n10 × 18 = 180\n210 + 180 = 390\n432 − 390 = 42\nOR\nAward ONE mark for sight of 420 (as evidence of the sum of the two correct products)."},
{"qimg":{"src":"2022-P2-Q21","w":1088,"h":1030},"n":"21","paperNum":"2","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2022","strand":"Algebra","subtopic":"Finding unknown values","marks":2,"ref":"6A4/6A1","intro":"Each shape stands for a number.\nThe total of the shapes on the diagonal line is 48\nThe total of all the shapes is 200","text":"Calculate the value of each shape.\n(a) triangle = ☐\n(b) circle = ☐","answer":"(a) 16&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;(b) 30","markscheme":"If the answer to part (b) is incorrect, award ONE mark for an answer of (200 – 5n) ÷ 4, where n represents the answer to part (a). The value of n must be between 12 and 18 (inclusive).\nAny follow-through fraction or decimal answer must be expressed as an exact value."},
{"qimg":{"src":"2022-P2-Q22","w":1088,"h":816},"n":"22","paperNum":"2","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2022","strand":"Ratio and proportion","subtopic":"Proportion problems","marks":2,"ref":"6R1","intro":"You can make green paint by mixing:\n• 250ml of blue paint\n• 1,150ml of yellow paint.\nStefan wants to make some of this green paint.\nHe uses 750ml of blue paint.","text":"How much green paint does he make?","answer":"Award TWO marks for the correct answer of 4,200","markscheme":"If the answer is incorrect, award ONE mark for evidence of an appropriate method, e.g.\n• 750 ÷ 250 = 3\n1,150 + 250 = 1,400\n1,400 × 3\nOR\n• 750 ÷ 250 = 3\n1,150 × 3 = 3,350 (error)\n3,350 + 750\nAdditional guidance:\nAnswer need not be obtained for the award of ONE mark.\nAward ONE mark for sight of 3450, 3.45 OR 3.450 (as evidence of correctly calculating how much yellow paint is required)."},
{"qimg":{"src":"2022-P2-Q23","w":1088,"h":1038},"n":"23","paperNum":"2","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2022","strand":"Measurement","subtopic":"Converting units","marks":2,"ref":"6M9/6M5","intro":"Adam has a bag of fruit that weighs 1.25 kilograms.\nHe takes out a banana. Now the bag of fruit weighs 1.1kg.\nNext, he takes out an orange. Now the bag weighs 920g.","text":"How much more does the orange weigh than the banana?","answer":"Award TWO marks for the correct answer of 30","markscheme":"If the answer is incorrect, award ONE mark for evidence of an appropriate method, e.g.\n• 1.25kg − 1.1kg = 0.05kg (error)\n1100g − 920g = 180g\n180 − 50 = 130g\nOR\nAward ONE mark for the correct weight of the banana and the orange, e.g.\n0.15(kg) AND 180(g)\nAdditional guidance:\nAccept for TWO marks 0.03kg for final answer in working and the answer box blank OR 0.03 in the answer box where the grams has been replaced with kilograms.\nAccept for ONE mark 0.03(g) in the answer box OR as the final answer in working and answer box blank.\nAnswer need not be obtained for the award of ONE mark.\nAny conversion of units must be correct. Do not award the mark for a method that contains an incorrect conversion, e.g. 1.25 − 1.1 = 0.16 (error), 1100 − 920 = 180, 180 − 16 (conversion error)."},
{"qimg":{"src":"2022-P2-Q24","w":1088,"h":1070},"n":"24","paperNum":"2","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2022","strand":"Properties of shapes","subtopic":"Angles","marks":2,"ref":"6G4a/5G4b","intro":"Here is an isosceles triangle inside a rectangle.","text":"Calculate the sizes of angles x and y.\nx = ☐°   y = ☐°","answer":"Award TWO marks for x = 75 AND y = 15","markscheme":"If the answer is incorrect, award ONE mark for evidence of an appropriate method calculating both angles, e.g.\n• 180 − 30 = 150\n150 ÷ 2 = 70 (error)\n90 − 70\nOR\nAward ONE mark for either correct x OR y.\nAdditional guidance:\nAnswer need not be obtained for the award of ONE mark.\nIf there is no evidence of an appropriate method and the values for x AND y are incorrect, accept for ONE mark x + y = 90, unless x is between 65–69 (inclusive) AND y is between 21–25 (inclusive)."},
{"qimg":{"src":"2022-P2-Q25","w":1088,"h":1250},"n":"25","paperNum":"2","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2022","strand":"Position and direction","subtopic":["Translation","Reflection"],"marks":2,"ref":"6P2","intro":"The triangle is to be transformed on the grid as follows:\n• First translate the shape 7 units down.\n• Then reflect the resulting triangle in the y-axis.","text":"Draw the new triangle on the grid after each transformation.","answer":"Award TWO marks for both triangles correctly drawn, as shown:","markscheme":"Accept slight inaccuracies in drawing provided the intention is clear.\nIgnore any triangles drawn in the 2nd quadrant, unless it is a correct follow-through of triangle A.\nAward ONE mark for either:\n• correct triangle A\nOR\n• correct triangle B\nOR\n• a correct reflection of an incorrectly translated triangle (maintaining congruency of the original triangle).","ansImg":{"src":"2022-P2-Q25-answer","w":492,"h":488}},
{"qimg":{"src":"2022-P3-Q1","w":1088,"h":602},"n":"1","paperNum":"3","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2022","strand":"Properties of shapes","subtopic":"3-D shapes","marks":1,"ref":"5G3b","text":"Here is a drawing of a hexagonal prism.\nHow many faces does the prism have?","answer":"8","markscheme":""},
{"qimg":{"src":"2022-P3-Q2","w":1088,"h":832},"n":"2","paperNum":"3","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2022","strand":"Calculation","subtopic":"Multiplication and division facts","marks":1,"ref":"4C6c/3C6","text":"Here are six number cards.\n3   4   5   6   7   8\nUse all six cards to complete the three multiplications below.\n24 = ☐ × ☐\n28 = ☐ × ☐\n30 = ☐ × ☐","answer":"Award ONE mark for all multiplications completed correctly with the given cards, as shown:","markscheme":"Accept for each multiplication the numbers given in either order, e.g.\n8 × 3\n7 × 4\n6 × 5","ansImg":{"src":"2022-P3-Q2-answer","w":234,"h":244}},
{"qimg":{"src":"2022-P3-Q3","w":1088,"h":890},"n":"3","paperNum":"3","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2022","strand":"Calculation","subtopic":"Missing number problems","marks":2,"ref":"3M9a","intro":"Olivia buys a banana, an apple and a bag of nuts.\nbanana 30p   apple 45p   bag of nuts 60p\nShe pays with three 50p coins.","text":"How much is her change?","answer":"Award TWO marks for the correct answer of 15(p)","markscheme":"If the answer is incorrect, award ONE mark for evidence of an appropriate method, e.g.\n• 30p + 45p + 60p = 135p\n50p × 3 − 135p\nOR\n• 50 − 30 = 20\n50 − 45 = 5\n20 + 5 + 50 = 75\n75 − 60\nOR\n• 150 − 45 = 95 (error)\n95 − 60 = 35\n35 − 30\nAdditional guidance:\nAnswer need not be obtained for the award of ONE mark.\nAccept for ONE mark an answer of 0.15(p) OR £15(p) as evidence of an appropriate method."},
{"qimg":{"src":"2022-P3-Q4","w":1088,"h":1056},"n":"4","paperNum":"3","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2022","strand":"Fractions, decimals and percentages","subtopic":"Fraction and decimal equivalents","marks":2,"ref":"4F6a/4F6b","text":"Draw four lines to match each fraction to its equivalent decimal.\n1/2   3/10   3/4   3/100\n0.3   0.5   0.8   0.03   0.25   0.75","answer":"Award TWO marks for all four fractions matched to the correct decimal as shown:","markscheme":"Lines need not touch the boxes, provided the intention is clear.\nDo not accept any fraction that has been matched to more than one decimal number.\nAward ONE mark for three fractions and decimals matched correctly.","ansImg":{"src":"2022-P3-Q4-answer","w":340,"h":440}},
{"qimg":{"src":"2022-P3-Q5","w":1088,"h":1038},"n":"5","paperNum":"3","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2022","strand":"Calculation","subtopic":"Missing number problems","marks":2,"ref":"3C4/3C2","intro":"Some children vote for their favourite ice-cream flavour.\nvanilla 87   chocolate 154   strawberry ?   mint 38   Total 402","text":"How many children vote for strawberry?","answer":"Award TWO marks for the correct answer of 123","markscheme":"If the answer is incorrect, award ONE mark for evidence of an appropriate method, e.g.\n• 87 + 154 + 38 = 279\n402 − 279\nOR\n• 87 + 154 + 38 = 269 (error)\n402 − 269\nAdditional guidance:\nAnswer need not be obtained for the award of ONE mark."},
{"qimg":{"src":"2022-P3-Q6","w":1088,"h":1288},"n":"6","paperNum":"3","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2022","strand":"Number and place value","subtopic":"Negative numbers","marks":2,"ref":"5N5/4S2","intro":"This chart shows the range of temperatures each day during one week from Monday to Friday.","text":"(a) What was the lowest temperature?\n(b) What was the difference between the highest and lowest temperatures on Wednesday?","answer":"(a) −7&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;(b) 8","markscheme":"6a: Do not accept 7−\n6b: Do not accept −8"},
{"qimg":{"src":"2022-P3-Q7","w":1088,"h":664},"n":"7","paperNum":"3","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2022","strand":"Calculation","subtopic":"Written addition and subtraction","marks":2,"ref":"5C4","intro":"One Saturday afternoon, a total of 234,869 people attended three rugby matches.\n• 80,978 people attended match 1\n• 72,319 people attended match 2","text":"How many people attended match 3?","answer":"Award TWO marks for the correct answer of 81,572","markscheme":"Award ONE mark for evidence of an appropriate method, e.g.\n• 80,978\n+ 72,319\n153,297\n234,869 − 153,297\nOR\n• 234,869\n− 80,978\n153,891\n153,891 − 72,319\nOR\n• 234,869\n− 72,319\n162,550\n162,550 − 80,978\nOR\nAward ONE mark for sight of 153,297 OR 153,891 OR 162,550\nAdditional guidance:\nAnswer need not be obtained for the award of ONE mark."},
{"qimg":{"src":"2022-P3-Q8","w":1088,"h":728},"n":"8","paperNum":"3","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2022","strand":"Number and place value","subtopic":"Rounding","marks":2,"ref":"4N4b","intro":"7,546","text":"Round this number:\nto the nearest 1,000\nto the nearest 100\nto the nearest 10","answer":"Award TWO marks for the correct three numbers, as shown:","markscheme":"Do not accept 500 or 50 for the second and third entries.\nIf the answer is incorrect, award ONE mark for any two of the numbers rounded correctly.","ansImg":{"src":"2022-P3-Q8-answer","w":454,"h":204}},
{"qimg":{"src":"2022-P3-Q9","w":1088,"h":250},"n":"9","paperNum":"3","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2022","strand":"Calculation","subtopic":"Multiplying and dividing by 10, 100 and 1000","marks":1,"ref":"5C6b","text":"Complete the calculation.\n1,000 × 416 = 10 × ☐","answer":"41,600","markscheme":""},
{"qimg":{"src":"2022-P3-Q10","w":1088,"h":1194},"n":"10","paperNum":"3","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2022","strand":"Algebra","subtopic":"Finding unknown values","marks":2,"ref":"5M9a/6A4","intro":"Adam buys 4 pens and a ruler and pays £4.75 altogether.\nJack buys 2 pens and pays £1.98 altogether.","text":"How much does a ruler cost?","answer":"Award TWO marks for the correct answer of 79(p) OR (£)0.79","markscheme":"If the answer is incorrect, award ONE mark for an appropriate method, e.g.\n• £4.75 − £1.98 = £2.77\n£2.77 − £1.98\nOR\n• 198 × 2 = 397p (error)\n£4.75 − 397p\nOR\n• £2 × 2 = £4\n£4.75 − £4 = 75p\n75p + 4p\nAdditional guidance:\nAnswer need not be obtained for the award of ONE mark.\nAccept for ONE mark an answer of 0.79p OR £79(p) as evidence of a correct method."},
{"qimg":{"src":"2022-P3-Q11","w":1088,"h":398},"n":"11","paperNum":"3","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2022","strand":"Calculation","subtopic":"Multiples","marks":1,"ref":"5C8a","intro":"Ally chooses a whole number.\nWhen she multiplies her number by 4, the answer is less than 100\nWhen she multiplies her number by 5, the answer is greater than 100","text":"Write a number that Ally could have started with.","answer":"Award ONE mark for:\n21 OR 22 OR 23 OR 24","markscheme":"Award ONE mark for more than one correct answer given and there are no incorrect answers.\nDo not accept decimal numbers."},
{"qimg":{"src":"2022-P3-Q12","w":1088,"h":1400},"n":"12","paperNum":"3","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2022","strand":"Calculation","subtopic":"Missing number problems","marks":2,"ref":"5C8b","intro":"William says the rule for this diagram.\nFind the difference between the numbers in the circles.\nDouble this to make the number in the square.\n(Example: circles 5 and 20 → square 30)","text":"Use the same rule to write the missing numbers below.\n(a) 32 and 100 → square = ☐\n(b) 110 and square 400 → circle = ☐","answer":"(a) 136&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;(b) 310 OR −90","markscheme":""},
{"qimg":{"src":"2022-P3-Q13","w":1088,"h":290},"n":"13","paperNum":"3","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2022","strand":"Fractions, decimals and percentages","subtopic":"Adding and subtracting fractions","marks":1,"ref":"5F4","text":"Write the missing fraction to make this addition correct.\n2/3 + ☐ = 5/6","answer":"1/6","markscheme":"Accept equivalent fractions or an exact decimal equivalent, e.g. 0.16̅ (accept any unambiguous indication of the recurring digits).\nDo not accept rounded or truncated decimals."},
{"qimg":{"src":"2022-P3-Q14","w":1088,"h":454},"n":"14","paperNum":"3","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2022","strand":"Algebra","subtopic":"Substitution and simple equations","marks":1,"ref":"6A2/5M9a","text":"Jack hires a hall for a party.\nThis formula is used to work out the total cost.\nTotal cost = £15 booking fee + £12.50 per hour\nWhat is the total cost of hiring the hall from 6pm until 11pm?","answer":"£77.50","markscheme":""},
{"qimg":{"src":"2022-P3-Q15","w":1088,"h":1322},"n":"15","paperNum":"3","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2022","strand":"Properties of shapes","subtopic":"Angles","marks":2,"ref":"5G4b/3G4b","intro":"Stefan stands in the centre of this square.\nStefan is facing towards F.\nHe turns anti-clockwise to face D.","text":"(a) What angle does Stefan turn through?\nStefan is now facing towards D.\nHe turns 3 right angles clockwise.\n(b) Write the letter he faces after the turn.","answer":"(a) 90&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;(b) B","markscheme":"15b: Accept alternative unambiguous indication of the correct answer."},
{"qimg":{"src":"2022-P3-Q16","w":1088,"h":1050},"n":"16","paperNum":"3","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2022","strand":"Fractions, decimals and percentages","subtopic":"Equivalent fractions","marks":2,"ref":"5F2b","text":"Part of this 10 × 10 grid is shaded.\nTick the fractions that represent the shaded part of the grid.\n1/4   2/5   4/10   6/10   40/100","answer":"Award TWO marks for three boxes ticked correctly, as shown:","markscheme":"Accept alternative unambiguous positive indication of the correct answer, e.g. Y.\nIf the answer is incorrect, award ONE mark for:\n• only two boxes ticked correctly and no incorrect boxes ticked.\nOR\n• three boxes ticked correctly and one incorrect box ticked.","ansImg":{"src":"2022-P3-Q16-answer","w":210,"h":342}},
{"qimg":{"src":"2022-P3-Q17","w":1088,"h":1208},"n":"17","paperNum":"3","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2022","strand":"Measurement","subtopic":"Perimeter","marks":2,"ref":"5M9a/5F5","intro":"Kim makes a cuboid model using straws.\nShe uses straws that are 7.5cm long for the height.\nShe uses straws that are 11cm long for the length.\nShe uses straws that are 8.5cm long for the width.","text":"What is the total length of all the straws in her model?","answer":"Award TWO marks for the correct answer of 108","markscheme":"Misreads are not allowed.\nIf the answer is incorrect, award ONE mark for an appropriate method, e.g.\n• 7.5 × 4 = 30\n11 × 4 = 44\n8.5 × 4 = 34\n30 + 44 + 34\nOR\n• 7.5 + 11 + 8.5 = 27\n27 × 4\nOR\n• 7.5 + 7.5 + 7.5 + 7.5 + 11 + 11 + 11 + 11 + 8.5 + 8.5 + 8.5 + 8.5\nAdditional guidance:\nAnswer need not be obtained for the award of ONE mark."},
{"qimg":{"src":"2022-P3-Q18","w":1088,"h":1052},"n":"18","paperNum":"3","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2022","strand":"Ratio and proportion","subtopic":"Percentages of amounts","marks":2,"ref":"6R2","intro":"The full price of a T-shirt is £15\nThe price is reduced by 30%.","text":"What is the reduced price?","answer":"Award TWO marks for the correct answer of (£)10.50","markscheme":"If the answer is incorrect, award ONE mark for evidence of an appropriate method, e.g.\n• 70 × 15 ÷ 100\nOR\n• 10 × 15 ÷ 100 = £1.50\n3 × £1.50 = £4.50\n£15 − £4.50\nOR\nAward ONE mark for sight of (£)4.50\nAdditional guidance:\nAnswer need not be obtained for the award of ONE mark.\nAward ONE mark for a final answer of (£)10.5 OR (£)105 OR (£)1050 as evidence of an appropriate method."},
{"qimg":{"src":"2022-P3-Q19","w":1088,"h":784},"n":"19","paperNum":"3","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2022","strand":["Prime numbers","Square numbers"],"subtopic":"Prime numbers","marks":1,"ref":"5C5b/5C5d","intro":"Jack says,\n\"When you square a prime number, the answer has only two factors.\"","text":"Explain why Jack is not correct.","answer":"Award ONE mark for a correct explanation, e.g.\n• It has 3 factors – the prime number, 1 and the square of the prime number.\n• The prime number has 2 factors; the squared prime number will be divisible by one, itself and the prime number.\n• All prime numbers squared have 3 factors.\nOR\nA correct explanation that gives a counter example, e.g.\n• 5 is prime\n5² = 25\n25 has 3 factors: 1, 5 and 25, not two\n• 7² has more than 2 factors – 1, 7 and 49\n• 11² = 1 × 121 = 11 × 11\n• 3² = 9\n9 – 1, 9, 3\n• 5² = 25\nFactors of 25 = 1, 5, 25\nAll squared primes have 3 factors.","markscheme":"Do not accept vague or incomplete explanations, e.g.\n• A square number doesn't have 2 factors (repeat of the question)\n• 2² = 4 (incomplete)\n• Prime numbers have 2 factors only (incomplete)\n• Prime numbers squared have more than 2 factors (vague)\nDo not accept explanations which include incorrect mathematics or incorrect information relevant to the explanation, e.g.\n• 49 = 1, 7, 49\n• 5 squared is 25, 1, 5, 5, 25, 25 has four factors\n• All prime numbers squared have more than 3 factors"},
{"qimg":{"src":"2022-P3-Q20","w":1088,"h":1352},"n":"20","paperNum":"3","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2022","strand":"Statistics","subtopic":"Mean","marks":3,"ref":"6S3/6C8/5N4","intro":"This table shows how many people finished the New York Marathon in each of the first four decades it was held.\n1st decade 24,863   2nd decade 170,932   3rd decade 282,420   4th decade 350,824","text":"What is the mean number of people who finished the marathon per decade? Round your answer to the nearest hundred.","answer":"Award THREE marks for the correct answer of 207,300","markscheme":"If the answer is incorrect, award TWO marks for:\n• evidence of an appropriate complete method which contains no more than one error, e.g.\n24,863\n170,932\n282,420\n+ 350,824\n828,939 (error)\n828,939 ÷ 4 = 207,234 r3\nRounded to the nearest hundred = 207,200\nOR\n• sight of 207,259 r3 OR 207,259 3/4 OR 207,259.75\nAward ONE mark for:\n• evidence of an appropriate method with more than one error.\nAdditional guidance:\nAnswer need not be obtained or rounded for the award of ONE mark.\nA misread of a number may affect the award of marks. No marks are awarded if there is more than one misread or if the mathematics is simplified.\nTWO marks will be awarded if an appropriate method with the misread number is followed through correctly.\nONE mark will be awarded for evidence of an appropriate method with the misread number followed through correctly with no more than one error."},

{"qimg":{"src":"2023-P2-Q5","w":1110,"h":1024},"n":"5","paperNum":"2","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2023","strand":"Calculation","subtopic":"Explaining calculations","marks":1,"ref":"6R4/6A5","text":"Cars and motorbikes are parked in a street (car = 4 wheels, motorbike = 2 wheels). Stefan counts 3 motorbikes and 5 cars. He counts 28 wheels altogether. Explain why Stefan cannot be correct.","answer":"Award ONE mark for a correct explanation that demonstrates why Stefan's total number of wheels is incorrect, e.g.\n\nUses 5 cars and 3 motorbikes to show that the total number of wheels cannot be 28 because there are 26 wheels, e.g.\n• 5 × 4 = 20\n  3 × 2 = 6\n  20 + 6 = 26 (not 28)\n\n• 20 and 6 - he is wrong because you need an extra pair of wheels.\n• because on 5 cars there are 20 wheels but on 3 motorbikes there are 6 wheels so he would need another motorbike to have 28 wheels.\n• 26 (not 28)\n\nOR\nUses 3 motorbikes and the total of 28 wheels to show that the number of cars cannot be 5, e.g.\n• 3 motorbikes would have 6 wheels which leaves 22 wheels for the cars. But 22 divided by 4 is five and a half cars, so that can't be possible.\n\nOR\nUses 5 cars and the total number of 28 wheels to show that the number of motorbikes cannot be 3, e.g.\n• There are 5 cars with 20 wheels. And there must be 4 motorbikes for him to have 28 wheels, so Stefan is wrong.\n\nOR\nDemonstrates that Stefan would have either two extra wheels or an extra motorbike, e.g.\n• He is wrong because he has counted 2 more wheels.","markscheme":"Do not accept vague or incomplete explanations, e.g.\n• 20 and 6\n• because 3 motorbikes is 6 wheels.\n• he is two off the answer.\n\nDo not accept responses that restate the question e.g. 3 motorbikes and 5 cars does not equal 28\n\nDo not accept explanations which include incorrect mathematics or incorrect information relevant to the explanation."},

{"qimg":{"src":"2023-P2-Q17","w":1154,"h":656},"n":"17","paperNum":"2","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2023","strand":"Calculation","subtopic":"Short division","marks":2,"ref":"5C8b/5C7b","text":"The manager of a flower shop orders 4 boxes of red roses. There are 50 roses in each box. The manager makes bunches with 6 roses in each bunch. What is the greatest number of bunches that can be made? (show your method)","answer":"Award TWO marks for the correct answer of 33","markscheme":"If the answer is incorrect, award ONE mark for evidence of an appropriate method, e.g.\n• 4 × 50 = 200\n  200 ÷ 6 = 30 (error)\n\nOR\n• 50 ÷ 6 = 8 r2\n  (8 r2) × 4 = 32 r8\n\nOR\nAward ONE mark for sight of:\n• 33 1/3 OR 33.3̇ OR 33.33r OR 33.3 OR 33 r2\n(as evidence of completing 200 ÷ 6 correctly without interpreting the remainder in context)\n\nAdditional guidance:\n\nAnswer need not be obtained for the award of ONE mark.\n\nIf the pupil reaches an answer with a remainder and subsequently rounds to the nearest integer value either side, then the method remains appropriate for the award of ONE mark, e.g.\n• 200 ÷ 6 = 31 r8\n\nAcceptable rounded answers would be 31 OR 32\n\nFor the 'sight of' mark, accept equivalent fractions.\n\nAward ONE mark for an answer of 34."},
{"qimg":{"src":"2023-P2-Q18","w":1110,"h":767},"n":"18","paperNum":"2","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2023","strand":"Fractions, decimals and percentages","subtopic":"Adding and subtracting fractions","marks":2,"ref":"5F4/6F2","text":"A cinema sells tickets at three different prices. 1/20 of the tickets are price A. 3/5 of the tickets are price B. The rest of the tickets are price C. What fraction of the tickets are price C? (show your method)","answer":"Award TWO marks for the correct answer of 7/20","markscheme":"If the answer is incorrect, award ONE mark for evidence of an appropriate method, e.g.\n• 3/5 = 12/20 (using (3×4)/(5×4))\n  1/20 + 12/20 = 13/20\n  1 − 13/20\n\n• 1/20 + 3/5 = 13/20\n  1 − 13/20\n\nOR\nAward ONE mark for sight of 13/20 (as evidence of correctly totalling price A and price B tickets).\n\nAccept for TWO marks an equivalent fraction of 7/20 e.g. 35/100\n\nAnswer need not be obtained for the award of ONE mark.\n\nAlso accept for ONE mark equivalent fractions for 13/20 e.g. 65/100"},
{"qimg":{"src":"2023-P2-Q19","w":1154,"h":223},"n":"19","paperNum":"2","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2023","strand":"Calculation","subtopic":"Missing number problems","marks":1,"ref":"5C8a/5C6a","text":"Write the missing number to make this division correct. 15,000 ÷ ▢ = 75","answer":"200","markscheme":""},

{"qimg":{"src":"2023-P2-Q21","w":1110,"h":825},"n":"21","paperNum":"2","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2023","strand":"Measurement","subtopic":"Converting units","marks":2,"ref":"5M6","text":"The height of the tallest person in history is 8 feet 11 inches. Conversion table: one foot = 30 centimetres, one inch = 2.5 centimetres. Use this conversion table to calculate the height of the tallest person, in centimetres. (show your method)","answer":"Award TWO marks for 267.5 OR 267 1/2 (cm)","markscheme":"If the answer is incorrect, award ONE mark for evidence of an appropriate method, e.g.\n• 30 × 8 = 210 (error)\n  2.5 × 11 = 27.5\n  210 + 27.5\n\nOR\n• 30 ÷ 2.5 = 12\n  8 × 12 + 11 = 106 (error)\n  106 × 2.5\n\nOR\n• 12 inches = 1 ft\n  1 ft + 8 ft = 9 ft\n  30 × 9 = 270\n  270 − 2.5\n\nAdditional guidance:\n\nAnswer need not be obtained for the award of ONE mark."},
{"qimg":{"src":"2023-P2-Q22","w":1154,"h":828},"n":"22","paperNum":"2","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2023","strand":"Fractions, decimals and percentages","subtopic":"Fractions of shapes","marks":1,"ref":"4F2/5F4","text":"Here is a regular hexagon. The area of the large shaded triangle is double the area of the small shaded triangle. What fraction of the whole hexagon is the shaded area?","answer":"3/12","markscheme":"Also accept equivalent fractions, e.g. 1/4"},
{"qimg":{"src":"2023-P2-Q23","w":1110,"h":785},"n":"23","paperNum":"2","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2023","strand":"Ratio and proportion","subtopic":"Percentages of amounts","marks":2,"ref":"6R2/6C8","text":"A small box contains 650 grams of cereal. A large box contains 20% more cereal. One portion of cereal is 40 grams. How many full portions are in a large box? (show your method)","answer":"Award TWO marks for the correct answer of 19","markscheme":"If the answer is incorrect, award ONE mark for evidence of an appropriate method, e.g.\n• 650 ÷ 10 = 65\n  65 × 2 = 130\n  650 + 130 = 780\n  780 ÷ 40\n\n• 20% of 650 = 130\n  130 + 650 = 770 (error)\n  770 ÷ 40\n\nOR\nAward ONE mark for sight of:\n• 19.5 OR 19 1/2 OR 19 r20 OR 19 r2\n(as evidence of a complete method before rounding down)\n\nAdditional guidance:\n\nAnswer need not be obtained for the award of ONE mark.\n\nIf a pupil's method uses repeated addition or subtraction appropriately, only one step error is allowed, otherwise the method is not appropriate.\n\nIf the pupil reaches an answer with a remainder and subsequently rounds to the nearest integer value either side, then the method remains appropriate for the award of ONE mark, e.g. 780 ÷ 40 = 14 r2 (error)\n\nAcceptable rounded answers would be 14 OR 15\n\nAward ONE mark for an answer of 20."},

{"qimg":{"src":"2023-P2-Q25","w":1110,"h":1198},"n":"25","paperNum":"2","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2023","strand":"Calculation","subtopic":"Multiplication and division facts","marks":3,"ref":"5C8a/5C6a","text":"There are 25 sheets of paper in a small pack. There are 500 sheets in a large pack. How many small packs make one large pack?\n\nThe mass of the paper in the large pack is 2.4 kilograms. What is the mass of one sheet of paper, in grams? (show your method)","answer":"25a: 20\n\n25b: Award TWO marks for the correct answer of 4.8 (g)","markscheme":"25b: If the answer is incorrect, award ONE mark for evidence of an appropriate method, e.g.\n• 2.4 × 1000 = 240 (error)\n  240 ÷ 500\n\n• 2.4 ÷ 500 = 0.0048\n  0.0048 × 1000\n\nAccept for TWO marks 0.0048 kg for final answer in working and the answer box blank OR 0.0048 in answer box where the grams has been replaced with kilograms (kg).\n\nAccept for ONE mark 0.0048g in the answer box OR as the final answer in the working and answer box blank.\n\nAdditional guidance:\n\nAnswer need not be obtained for award of ONE mark."},

{"qimg":{"src":"2023-P3-Q9","w":1154,"h":868},"n":"9","paperNum":"3","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2023","strand":"Calculation","subtopic":"Explaining calculations","marks":1,"ref":"3C6","text":"Jack says: 'I multiplied a whole number by 3. My answer was 32.' Explain why Jack is not correct.","answer":"Award ONE mark for an explanation that recognises that 32 is not a multiple of 3, e.g.\n• 32 is not in the 3× table\n• 32 ÷ 3 = 10 r2 or 10.66 (which are not whole numbers)\n• if you count in multiples of 3 from 0, you won't get 32\n• 3 + 2 = 5, 5 is not a multiple of 3 so he is wrong.\n\nOR\nFor a description that includes one or both of the multiples of 3 either side of 32, e.g.\n• if you do 10 × 3 = 30 and 11 × 3 = 33 there is no 32\n• 10 × 3 = 30 and 32 is 2 away.","markscheme":"Do not accept responses that restate the question, e.g. Jack is not correct because if you multiply 3 by any whole number you will not get 32.\n\nDo not accept vague or incomplete explanations, e.g.\n• If you multiply by 3 you will get 30, not 32\n• 3, 6, 9, 12, 15, 18, 21, 24, 27, 30, 33\n• 32 is not a factor of 3\n\nDo not accept explanations which include incorrect mathematics or incorrect information relevant to the explanation."},
{"qimg":{"src":"2023-P3-Q10","w":1110,"h":244},"n":"10","paperNum":"3","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2023","strand":"Calculation","subtopic":"Square numbers","marks":1,"ref":"5C5d","text":"Write the missing square number to make this addition correct: 8² + ▢² = 73","answer":"3","markscheme":"Accept the answer of 9 as long as the exponent has been crossed out."},
{"qimg":{"src":"2023-P3-Q11","w":1110,"h":711},"n":"11","paperNum":"3","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2023","strand":"Calculation","subtopic":"Written addition and subtraction","marks":2,"ref":"4C4","text":"At the start of April, a shop had 15,000 games. The shop sold 7,918 games in April and 4,624 games in May. How many games did the shop have left at the end of May? (show your method)","answer":"Award TWO marks for correct answer of 2,458","markscheme":"If the answer is incorrect, award ONE mark for evidence of an appropriate method, e.g.\n• 7,918 + 4,624 = 12,542\n  15,000 − 12,542\n\nOR\n• 15,000 − 7,918 = 7,182 (error)\n  7,182 − 4,624\n\nOR\n• 15,000 − 4,624 = 10,376\n  10,376 − 7,918 = 2,558 (error)\n\nAdditional guidance:\n\nAnswer need not be obtained for the award of ONE mark."},
{"qimg":{"src":"2023-P3-Q12","w":1154,"h":1192},"n":"12","paperNum":"3","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2023","strand":"Properties of shapes","subtopic":"Nets","marks":2,"ref":"6G3b","text":"This is a drawing of a (tall, thin) cuboid. Tick the nets that could make the cuboid (four net options shown).","answer":"Award TWO marks for two boxes correctly ticked, and no incorrect boxes ticked: the 2nd net (T-shaped with one extra square) and the 4th net are correct.","markscheme":"Accept alternative unambiguous positive indication of the correct answer.\n\nIf the answer is incorrect, award ONE mark for:\n• two boxes ticked correctly and one incorrect box ticked.\n\nOR\n• only one box ticked correctly and no incorrect boxes ticked."},

{"qimg":{"src":"2023-P3-Q14","w":1110,"h":1022},"n":"14","paperNum":"3","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2023","strand":"Fractions, decimals and percentages","subtopic":"Fraction and decimal equivalents","marks":2,"ref":"5F11/5F12","text":"Here are five digit cards: 1, 2, 3, 4, 5. Use two cards to make a fraction equivalent to 25%.\n\nUse two cards to make a fraction equivalent to 0.4.","answer":"14a: 1/4\n\n14b: 2/5","markscheme":"Do not accept equivalent fractions (for either part)."},
{"qimg":{"src":"2023-P3-Q15","w":1154,"h":1171},"n":"15","paperNum":"3","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2023","strand":"Measurement","subtopic":"Telling the time","marks":2,"ref":"3M4d/3M4f/4M4b","text":"Amina went to a concert one evening. It took her an hour and twenty minutes to get there from home. She arrived at ten past seven. At what time did she leave home?\n\nThe concert started at 7:20 pm. It finished at 9:05 pm. How long did the concert last?","answer":"15a: 5:50, (0)5:50 pm OR 17:50\n\n15b: 1 (hour) 45 (minutes)","markscheme":"15a: Accept answer in words, e.g. ten to six, OR an answer written unconventionally, e.g. 10 to 6.\n\n15b: Award the mark if the answer is given in hours only or minutes only, i.e. 1.75 (hours) with minutes left blank, OR minutes only as 105 with hours left blank."},
{"qimg":{"src":"2023-P3-Q16","w":1110,"h":865},"n":"16","paperNum":"3","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2023","strand":"Calculation","subtopic":"Long division","marks":2,"ref":"6C7b/6C8/6C6","text":"A box of 24 chocolate eggs has a mass of 870 grams. The empty box has a mass of 30 grams. What is the mass of one chocolate egg? (show your method)","answer":"Award TWO marks for correct answer of 35(g)","markscheme":"If the answer is incorrect, award ONE mark for evidence of an appropriate method, e.g.\n• 870 − 30 = 840\n  840 ÷ 24\n\nOR\n• 870 − 30 = 850 (error)\n  850 ÷ 24 = 35 r10\n\nAdditional guidance:\n\nAnswer need not be obtained for the award of ONE mark.\n\nIf the pupil reaches an answer with a remainder and subsequently rounds to the nearest integer value either side, then the method remains appropriate for the award of ONE mark, e.g. 840 ÷ 24 = 36 r10\n\nAcceptable rounded answers would be 36 OR 37"},
{"qimg":{"src":"2023-P3-Q17","w":1154,"h":845},"n":"17","paperNum":"3","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2023","strand":"Fractions, decimals and percentages","subtopic":"Fractions of shapes","marks":1,"ref":"6F4/6F11","text":"This rectangle is divided into three parts, A, B and C (not to scale). Part A is 1/2 of the area of the rectangle. Part B is 1/3 of the area of the rectangle. What fraction of the area of the rectangle is shaded?","answer":"5/6","markscheme":"Accept equivalent fractions, e.g. 10/12"},
{"qimg":{"src":"2023-P3-Q18","w":1154,"h":1839},"n":"18","paperNum":"3","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2023","strand":"Statistics","subtopic":["Bar charts","Mean"],"marks":3,"ref":"5S1/4S1","text":"This table shows the total rainfall and sunshine each year at Heathrow Airport from 2010 to 2015. Use this table to complete the graph (draw the missing 2012 bar). Use a ruler.\n\nUse the table to calculate the mean hours of sunshine for Heathrow Airport from 2013 to 2015. (show your method)","answer":"18a: Award ONE mark for drawing the bar in the range of 650 mm to 750 mm, e.g.\n\n18b: Award TWO marks for the correct answer of 1,543","markscheme":"18a: Ignore the width of the bar.\n\n18b: If the answer is incorrect, award ONE mark for evidence of an appropriate method, e.g.\n• 1,452 + 1,669 + 1,508 = 4,629\n  4,629 ÷ 3\n\nOR\n• 1,452 + 1,669 + 1,508 = 4619 (error)\n  4619 ÷ 3\n\nOR\nAward ONE mark for sight of 4629 (as evidence of the sum of sunshine hours)\n\nAdditional guidance:\n\nAnswer need not be obtained or rounded for the award of ONE mark.\n\nAny acceptable rounding or truncating does not negate an appropriate method. Any value which does not result from correct rounding or truncating implies an additional step not shown.","ansImg":{"src":"2023-P3-Q18a-answer","w":492,"h":356}},
{"qimg":{"src":"2023-P3-Q19","w":1110,"h":982},"n":"19","paperNum":"3","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2023","strand":"Measurement","subtopic":"Money problems","marks":2,"ref":"5M9c/5M9a","text":"These are the prices of some vegetables in a shop: mushrooms £3.20 for 1 kg, carrots 60p for 1 kg. Layla buys 500 grams of mushrooms and 1 1/4 kg of carrots. She pays with a £5 note. How much change does Layla get? (show your method)","answer":"Award TWO marks for the correct answer of (£)2.65","markscheme":"If the answer is incorrect, award ONE mark for evidence of a complete method which contains no more than one arithmetic error, e.g.\n• £3.20 ÷ 2 = £1.60\n  1/4 of 60p = 15p\n  60p + 15p = 75p\n  £1.60 + 75p = £2.25 (error)\n  £5 − £2.25 = £2.75\n\nOR\n• sight of (£)2.35 OR 235(p) (as evidence of the total cost of mushrooms and carrots).\n\nAdditional guidance:\n\nMisreads are not allowed.\n\nAccept for ONE mark an answer of £265, £265p or £2,65 as evidence of an appropriate method."},
{"qimg":{"src":"2023-P3-Q20","w":1154,"h":956},"n":"20","paperNum":"3","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2023","strand":"Algebra","subtopic":"Expressions and formulae","marks":2,"ref":"6A1/4M7a","text":"The length of this rectangle is 6 cm. The width is w cm (not actual size). Circle all the methods below that can be used to work out the perimeter of the rectangle: w × 6, w × 2 + 12, 2 × (w + 6), 6 + w + 6 + w","answer":"Award TWO marks for the three correct expressions circled, as shown:","markscheme":"Accept alternative unambiguous positive indication of the correct answers.\n\nAward ONE mark for two correct expressions circled and no incorrect expressions circled.","ansImg":{"src":"2023-P3-Q20-answer","w":492,"h":256}},
{"qimg":{"src":"2023-P3-Q21","w":1110,"h":892},"n":"21","paperNum":"3","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2023","strand":"Ratio and proportion","subtopic":"Percentages of amounts","marks":3,"ref":"6R2","text":"There are 25 classes in a school. Each class has 34 pupils. 62% of all the pupils play a sport after school. What number of pupils do not play a sport? (show your method)","answer":"Award THREE marks for the correct answer of 323","markscheme":"Award TWO marks for:\n• An incorrect answer with evidence of an appropriate complete method with no more than one arithmetic error, e.g.\n  25\n  × 34\n  100\n  750\n  950 (error)\n  62% of 950 = 589\n  950 − 589 = 361\n\nOR\n• 34 × 25 = 950 (error)\n  95 × 3 = 285\n  9.5 × 8 = 76\n  285 + 76 = 361\n\nOR\n• sight of 527 (as evidence of calculating 62% of 850)\n\nA misread of a number may affect the award of marks. No marks are awarded if there is more than one misread or if the mathematics is simplified.\n\nTWO marks will be awarded if an appropriate method with the misread number is followed through correctly. ONE mark will be awarded for evidence of an appropriate method with the misread number followed through correctly with no more than one error.\n\nWithin an appropriate method, if the pupil has rounded appropriately with no more than one arithmetic error, the pupil may be awarded TWO marks.\n\nAward ONE mark for:\n• evidence of an appropriate method with more than one error.\n\nOR\n• sight of 850 (as evidence of the multiplication step completed correctly)\n\nAdditional guidance:\n\nAnswer need not be obtained for the award of ONE mark."},
{"qimg":{"src":"2023-P3-Q22","w":1154,"h":786},"n":"22","paperNum":"3","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2023","strand":"Algebra","subtopic":"Function machines","marks":1,"ref":"6A3/6G2a","text":"Megan uses number machines to calculate how many diagonals different shapes have (triangle: 3 → ×0 → ÷2 → 0; quadrilateral: 4 → ×1 → ÷2 → 2; pentagon: 5 → ×2 → ÷2 → 5). {"qimg":{"src":"2024-P2-Q6","w":1116,"h":528},"n":"6","paperNum":"2","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2024","strand":"Calculation","subtopic":"Missing number problems","marks":1,"ref":"3N6/3N2a/3N3","text":"Olivia is thinking of a number.\nMy number:\n• is greater than 236\n• is less than 245\n• has a 3 in the tens’ place\n• is an even number\nWhat number is Olivia thinking of?","answer":"238","markscheme":""},

Each packet holds 25 envelopes.\nHow many envelopes does the box hold?","answer":"1,000","markscheme":""},
{"qimg":{"src":"2024-P2-Q8","w":1116,"h":509},"n":"8","paperNum":"2","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2024","strand":"Number and place value","subtopic":"Rounding","marks":1,"ref":"5N4/4N4b","text":"Write a whole number in each box to make the statements correct.\nOne has been done for you.\n18 rounded to the nearest ten is 20\n☐ rounded to the nearest thousand is 4,000\n☐ rounded to the nearest ten thousand is 820,000","answer":"Any whole number in the range 3,500 – 4,499 inclusive\n\nAny whole number in the range 815,000 – 824,999 inclusive","markscheme":"Both numbers must be correct for the award of the mark."},
{"qimg":{"src":"2024-P2-Q9","w":1116,"h":564},"n":"9","paperNum":"2","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2024","strand":"Fractions, decimals and percentages","subtopic":"Multiplying and dividing decimals","marks":1,"ref":"4F9","text":"4 ÷ 10        40 ÷ 10\n4 ÷ 100      40 ÷ 100\nTwo of these calculations have the same answer.\nWrite this answer as a decimal.","answer":"0.4","markscheme":""},
{"qimg":{"src":"2024-P2-Q10","w":1116,"h":216},"n":"10","paperNum":"2","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2024","strand":"Calculation","subtopic":"Prime numbers","marks":1,"ref":"6C5","text":"Circle the two prime numbers that have a difference of 2\n15   17   19   21   23   25","answer":"17 and 19","markscheme":"Accept alternative unambiguous positive indication of the correct answer."},
{"qimg":{"src":"2024-P2-Q11","w":1116,"h":569},"n":"11","paperNum":"2","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2024","strand":"Ratio and proportion","subtopic":"Proportion problems","marks":1,"ref":"6R1/4C6a","text":"This table shows the number of children and adults at a childcare centre.\nComplete the table to make it correct.\nThe first row has been done for you.\n1 and under: 12 children, 4 adults, 3 children per adult\n2 or 3: 20 children, ☐ adults, 4 children per adult\n4 or 5: ☐ children, 3 adults, 8 children per adult","answer":"2 or 3: 5 adults\n\n4 or 5: 24 children","markscheme":""},
{"qimg":{"src":"2024-P2-Q12","w":1116,"h":1266},"n":"12","paperNum":"2","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2024","strand":"Fractions, decimals and percentages","subtopic":"Fractions of shapes","marks":1,"ref":"5F2b/4M7b","text":"Shapes W, X, Y and Z cover different fractions of this 10 by 10 square.\nMatch each shape to the correct fraction.","answer":"Shape W → 1/100\n\nShape X → 1/4\n\nShape Y → 2/5\n\nShape Z → 1/10","markscheme":"Lines need not touch the labels or fractions, provided the intention is clear.\n\nDo not accept any label matched to more than one fraction."},
{"qimg":{"src":"2024-P2-Q13","w":1116,"h":509},"n":"13","paperNum":"2","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2024","strand":"Properties of shapes","subtopic":"3-D shapes","marks":1,"ref":"6G2b/6G2a","text":"Match the name of each 3-D shape to its number of vertices.\ncube, square-based pyramid, triangular-based prism, octagonal-based pyramid","answer":"cube → 9\n\nsquare-based pyramid → 8\n\ntriangular-based prism → 6\n\noctagonal-based pyramid → 5","markscheme":"Lines need not touch the shape names or numbers, provided the intention is clear.\n\nDo not accept a shape name matched to more than one number."},
{"qimg":{"src":"2024-P2-Q14","w":1116,"h":384},"n":"14","paperNum":"2","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2024","strand":"Fractions, decimals and percentages","subtopic":"Fraction and decimal problems","marks":1,"ref":"4F10a/3F4/4C6a","text":"A class votes for a captain.\nThree-quarters of the class vote for Sam.\nThe remaining 7 pupils vote for Alex.\nHow many pupils are in the class?","answer":"28","markscheme":""},
{"qimg":{"src":"2024-P2-Q15","w":1116,"h":211},"n":"15","paperNum":"2","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2024","strand":"Fractions, decimals and percentages","subtopic":"Multiplying and dividing decimals","marks":1,"ref":"6F9a","text":"Write the missing number to make this multiplication correct.\n3.207 × 100 = ☐ × 10","answer":"32.07","markscheme":""},
{"qimg":{"src":"2024-P2-Q16","w":1116,"h":600},"n":"16","paperNum":"2","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2024","strand":"Number and place value","subtopic":"Place value","marks":2,"ref":"6N3","text":"Here is a number.\n9,658,214\nTick the statements that are true.\nThe digit 5 represents 50,000\nThe value of the digit 9 is nine hundred thousands.\nThe digit 6 represents 6 millions.\nThe value of the digit 2 is twenty tens.","answer":"Award TWO marks for two boxes ticked correctly, and no incorrect boxes ticked:\n‘The digit 5 represents 50,000’\n‘The value of the digit 2 is twenty tens.’","markscheme":"Accept alternative unambiguous positive indication of the correct answer.\n\nIf the answer is incorrect, award ONE mark for:\n• two boxes ticked correctly and one incorrect box ticked\nOR\n• only one box ticked correctly and no incorrect boxes ticked."},
{"qimg":{"src":"2024-P2-Q17","w":1116,"h":1059},"n":"17","paperNum":"2","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2024","strand":"Measurement","subtopic":"Money problems","marks":2,"ref":"5M9a/4F10b/3C4","text":"Chen buys these four items.\nApples 670 grams £1.50, Bread 400 grams £0.70, Butter 500 grams, Wheat O’Tees 750 grams £1.45\nChen pays for the four items with a £10 note. The price of the butter is not shown.\nShe receives £3.85 change.\nWhat is the price of the butter?","answer":"Award TWO marks for the correct answer of £2.50","markscheme":"If the answer is incorrect, award ONE mark for evidence of an appropriate complete method which contains no more than ONE arithmetic error, e.g.\n• £1.50 + £0.70 + £1.45 = £3.65\n£10.00 − £3.65 = £6.15 (error)\n£6.15 − £3.85 = £2.30\nOR\n• £1.50 + £0.70 + £1.45 + £3.85 = £7.50\n£10.00 − £7.50 = £3.50 (error)\n\nIf no final answer is given, all calculations within an appropriate method must be evaluated correctly for the award of ONE mark, e.g.\n• £1.50 + £0.70 + £1.45 = £3.65\n£10.00 − £3.65 = £6.35\n£6.35 − £3.85\n\nAdditional guidance:\nAccept for ONE mark an answer of £250, £250p, £2,50 or £2.5 as evidence of an appropriate method.\nMisreads of £3.85 as £3.65 OR miscopies of £3.65 as £3.85 are not allowed."},
{"qimg":{"src":"2024-P2-Q18","w":1116,"h":324},"n":"18","paperNum":"2","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2024","strand":"Measurement","subtopic":"Reading scales","marks":1,"ref":"6M5/4N4a","text":"Draw an arrow on the scale below to show 1350 grams.","answer":"Award ONE mark for an arrow drawn to 1.35kg.","markscheme":"Accept alternative unambiguous positive indication of the correct answer."},
{"qimg":{"src":"2024-P2-Q19","w":1116,"h":639},"n":"19","paperNum":"2","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2024","strand":"Calculation","subtopic":"Written addition and subtraction","marks":2,"ref":"4C4","text":"A hall has 1,250 seats.\nAt 7pm, 880 seats are filled.\nAt 8pm, there are 40 empty seats.\nHow many seats were filled between 7pm and 8pm?","answer":"Award TWO marks for the correct answer of 330","markscheme":"If the answer is incorrect, award ONE mark for evidence of an appropriate method, e.g.\n• 1,250 − 40 = 1,210\n1,210 − 880\nOR\n• 1,250 − 880 = 370\n370 − 40\nOR\n• 880 + 40 = 920\n1,250 − 920\n\nAdditional guidance:\nAnswer need not be obtained for the award of ONE mark."},
{"qimg":{"src":"2024-P2-Q20","w":1116,"h":650},"n":"20","paperNum":"2","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2024","strand":"Measurement","subtopic":"Telling the time","marks":2,"ref":"5M4/4M5","text":"Each day, a school has break from 10:15am to 10:30am and lunchtime from 12:40pm to 1:30pm.\nWhat is the total time the school has for breaks and lunchtime in a 5-day week?","answer":"Award TWO marks for the correct answer of 5 hours 25 minutes","markscheme":"If the answer is incorrect, award ONE mark for evidence of an appropriate method, e.g.\n• 10:15 to 10:30 = 15 mins\n12:40 to 1:30 = 50 mins\n15 + 50 = 65 mins\n65 × 5 = 320 mins (error)\n320 ÷ 60\nOR\n• 10:15 to 10:30 = 15 mins\n15 mins × 5 = 75 mins\n12:40 to 1:30 = 50 mins\n50 mins × 5 = 260 mins (error)\n75 mins + 260 mins = 335 mins\n335 ÷ 60 = 5 hrs 35 mins\n\nAward ONE mark for sight of:\n• 75 AND 250 (as evidence of an appropriate method)\nOR\n• 325 (minutes)\n\nAdditional guidance:\nAnswer need not be obtained for the award of ONE mark.\nAccept for ONE mark a correct answer given in hours OR minutes only, written either as a mixed number fraction or an exact decimal equivalent, e.g. 5 25/60 (hours) OR 5.416 (hours) OR 325 (minutes)."},
{"qimg":{"src":"2024-P2-Q21","w":1116,"h":774},"n":"21","paperNum":"2","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2024","strand":"Properties of shapes","subtopic":"Parallel and perpendicular lines","marks":1,"ref":"3G2","text":"Tick all the correct statements.\nAB is parallel to CD\nGH is parallel to AB\nCD is perpendicular to GH\nEF is perpendicular to CD","answer":"‘GH is parallel to AB’ and ‘CD is perpendicular to GH’","markscheme":"Accept alternative unambiguous positive indication of the correct answer."},
{"qimg":{"src":"2024-P2-Q22","w":1116,"h":1053},"n":"22","paperNum":"2","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2024","strand":"Statistics","subtopic":"Mean","marks":2,"ref":"6S3/3S1/5F10","text":"This table shows the distance that five friends travel to school each day.\nAmina 1.8km, William 2.4km, Layla 3.2km, Chen 1.6km, Dev 4.5km\nWhat is the mean distance they travel to school each day?","answer":"Award TWO marks for the correct answer of 2.7","markscheme":"If the answer is incorrect, award ONE mark for evidence of an appropriate method, e.g.\n• 1.8 + 2.4 + 3.2 + 1.6 + 4.5 = 13.5\n13.5 ÷ 5\n\nAdditional guidance:\nAny correct rounding or truncating of the answer does not negate an appropriate method.\nAny answer which does not result from correct rounding or truncating implies an additional step not shown."},
{"qimg":{"src":"2024-P2-Q23","w":1116,"h":644},"n":"23","paperNum":"2","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2024","strand":"Fractions, decimals and percentages","subtopic":"Fraction and decimal problems","marks":2,"ref":"6C7b/6F2","text":"Mrs Mills has 940 seeds to plant into trays.\nShe plants 12 seeds in each tray.\nThe last tray is not full.\nWhat fraction of the last tray is filled?","answer":"Award TWO marks for the correct answer of 4/12","markscheme":"Accept for TWO marks an exact equivalent fraction, e.g. 1/3\n\nIf the answer is incorrect, award ONE mark for evidence of an appropriate complete method, e.g.\n• 940 ÷ 12 = 78 remainder 5 (error)\n5 out of 12 = 5/12\nOR\n• [[img:2024-P2-Q23-a:224:102]]\nFinal answer = 2/12\n\nAward ONE mark for sight of:\n• 78 r4\nOR\n• 78.3 OR 78.33\nOR\n• 0.33 OR 33/100"},
{"qimg":{"src":"2024-P2-Q24","w":1116,"h":771},"n":"24","paperNum":"2","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2024","strand":"Calculation","subtopic":"Multiples","marks":2,"ref":"6C5/5C5d","text":"Here are four numbers.\n40   60   64   100\nUse each number once to complete these statements.\n☐ is a square number.\n☐ is a cube number.\n☐ is a common multiple of 4 and 5\n☐ is a common factor of 80 and 120","answer":"Award TWO marks for all boxes completed correctly:\n100 is a square number.\n64 is a cube number.\n60 is a common multiple of 4 and 5\n40 is a common factor of 80 and 120","markscheme":"Do not accept any numbers not given in the question.\n\nIf the answer is incorrect, award ONE mark for any three correct statements, as long as each of the three correct statements has a different number.\n\nDo not accept any statement where more than one number is given."},
{"qimg":{"src":"2024-P2-Q25","w":1116,"h":364},"n":"25","paperNum":"2","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2024","strand":"Algebra","subtopic":"Finding unknown values","marks":2,"ref":"6A4/4C3","text":"Write the missing numbers so that 3 × b – a = 2\na | 2 | ☐\nb | ☐ | 13","answer":"Award TWO marks for both boxes completed correctly:\na = 4 (when b = 2)\n\nb = 5 (when a = 13)","markscheme":"If the answer is incorrect, award ONE mark for one box completed correctly."},
{"qimg":{"src":"2024-P2-Q26","w":1116,"h":417},"n":"26","paperNum":"2","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2024","strand":"Position and direction","subtopic":"Translation","marks":1,"ref":"6P3/4P2","text":"Here are 3 translations on a coordinate grid.\nTick the translations that are four units to the left.\nfrom (0, 2) to (4, 2)\nfrom (6, 8) to (2, 8)\nfrom (−3, 5) to (−7, 5)","answer":"‘from (6, 8) to (2, 8)’ and ‘from (−3, 5) to (−7, 5)’","markscheme":"Accept alternative unambiguous positive indication of the correct answer."},
{"qimg":{"src":"2024-P2-Q27","w":1116,"h":1365},"n":"27","paperNum":"2","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2024","strand":"Ratio and proportion","subtopic":"Percentages of amounts","marks":1,"ref":"6R2","text":"Olivia has two jars of beads. The number of beads in Jar A is double the number of beads in Jar B.\nOlivia says, ‘25% of the number of beads in Jar A is the same as 50% of the number of beads in Jar B.’\nExplain why Olivia is correct.","answer":"Award ONE mark for an explanation that shows that the two quantities are equal, e.g. if there are 100 beads in Jar A and 50 beads in Jar B, 25% of A is 25 which is the same as 50% of 50","markscheme":"Do not accept responses that restate the question, e.g. ‘Because Jar B is half the amount of Jar A.’\n\nDo not accept vague or incomplete explanations, e.g. ‘25 + 25 = 50’\n\nDo not accept explanations which include incorrect mathematics or incorrect information that is relevant to the explanation."},
{"qimg":{"src":"2024-P3-Q1","w":1116,"h":1213},"n":"1","paperNum":"3","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2024","strand":"Fractions, decimals and percentages","subtopic":"Fractions of shapes","marks":1,"ref":"3F2","text":"These shapes have a fraction shaded.\nMatch each shaded fraction of a circle to the same shaded fraction of a rectangle.\nOne has been done for you.","answer":"Award ONE mark for all three pairs of shaded fractions matched correctly, as shown:","markscheme":"Lines need not touch the shaded fractions, provided the intention is clear.\n\nDo not accept a shaded circle matched to more than one shaded rectangle.","ansImg":{"src":"2024-P3-Q1-answer","w":492,"h":640}},

{"qimg":{"src":"2024-P3-Q3","w":1116,"h":1018},"n":"3","paperNum":"3","paper":"Papers 2 & 3 (reasoning)","sitting":"2024","strand":"Measurement","subtopic":"Money problems","marks":2,"ref":"4F10b/3M9a/4M9","text":"Jack buys milk and orange juice from a shop.\nmilk £1.45, orange juice £2.40\nHe pays with a £5 note.\nHow much change does Jack get?","answer":"Award TWO marks for the correct answer of £1.15","markscheme":"If the answer is incorrect, award ONE mark for evidence of an appropriate method, e.g.\n• £1.45 + £2.40 = £3.85\n£5.00 − £3.85\nOR\n• £5.00 − £1.45 = £3.55\n£3.55 − £2.40\n\nAdditional guidance:\nAnswer need not be obtained for the award of ONE mark.\nAccept for ONE mark an answer of £115, £115p or £1,15 as evidence of an appropriate method."},
